\displaystyle\leq\left(\alpha^{2}-2\alpha+2\right)^{d_{\mathcal{X}}-r}\leq\left(\alpha^{2}-2\alpha+2\right)^{(1-\alpha/2)d_{{\mathcal{X}}}},

which proves the bound.

Appendix 11.B
Proof of Lemma
5.10

Proof

Let
f_{i}=\sum_{j}u_{ij}s_{j}\mu_{j}
, and
{\bm{U}}=(u_{ij})=[{\bm{u}}_{1},\cdots,{\bm{u}}_{d}]
.

{\bm{U}}
is a matrix with
d
columns and infinitely many rows.
Then, since
{\left\langle f_{i},f_{j}\right\rangle}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}={\mathbb{I}}[i=j]
, we have
{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}={\bm{I}}_{d}
.
Let
{\bm{M}}(x)=[s_{1}\mu_{1}(x),s_{2}\mu_{2}(x),\cdots]
, and for a set of samples
S={\left\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{m}\right\}}
denote
{\bm{M}}_{j}={\bm{M}}(x_{j})
.
Then, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{S}({\mathcal{F}}_{d})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}}{\left[\sup_{F\in{\mathcal{F}}_{d}}\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{i}F(x_{j})\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}}{\left[\sup_{{\bm{U}}:{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}={\bm{I}}_{d}}{\left|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sum_{i=1}^{d}\sigma_{j}{\bm{u}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{u}}_{i}\right|}\right]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}}{\left[\sup_{{\bm{U}}:{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}={\bm{I}}_{d}}{\left|\Tr{\left\{{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right)}{\bm{U}}\right\}}\right|}\right]}

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}}{\left[\sup_{{\bm{U}}:{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}={\bm{I}}_{d}}{\left|\Tr{\left\{{\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right)}{\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\}}\right|}\right]},

where
\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}
are Rademacher variables, which are
i.i.d.
uniform random variables taking values in
{\left\{-1,+1\right\}}
.
Let
\beta_{1}\geq\beta_{2}\geq\cdots
be the singular values of
\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}
.
For any
x
,
{\bm{M}}(x)^{\top}{\bm{M}}(x)=\sum s_{i}^{2}\mu_{i}^{2}(x)\leq\kappa^{2}
, which implies that
{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{j}\leq\kappa^{2}
.

For any
{\bm{U}}
, the singular values of
{\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{\top}
are
d
ones and lots of zeros.
Moreover,
{\left\|\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right\|}_{F}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\beta_{i}^{2}
.
So by von Neumann’s trace inequality, we have

\sup_{{\bm{U}}:{\bm{U}}^{\top}{\bm{U}}={\bm{I}}_{d}}{\left|\Tr{\left\{{\left(\frac{1}{m}\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right)}{\bm{U}}{\bm{U}}^{\top}\right\}}\right|}\leq\sum_{i=1}^{d}\beta_{i}\leq\sqrt{d\sum_{i=1}^{d}\beta_{i}^{2}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{m}{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right\|}_{F}.

Thus, for any
S
, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{S}({\mathcal{F}}_{d})

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{m}\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}}{\left[{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right\|}_{F}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{d}}{m}\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}}{\left[\Tr{\left\{{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right)}^{\top}{\left(\sum_{l=1}^{m}\sigma_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}^{\top}\right)}\right\}}^{1/2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{m}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{m}}{\left[\Tr{\left(\sum_{j,l=1}^{m}\sigma_{j}\sigma_{l}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}^{\top}\right)}\right]}}\qquad(\textrm{Jensen})

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{d}}{m}\sqrt{\Tr{\left(\sum_{j,l=1}^{m}\mathbb{E}[\sigma_{j}\sigma_{l}]{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}^{\top}\right)}}

\displaystyle=\frac{\sqrt{d}}{m}\sqrt{\Tr{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right)}}=\frac{\sqrt{d}}{m}\sqrt{\sum_{j=1}^{m}{\left({\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{j}\right)}^{2}}\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{m}}\kappa^{2}.

Since
\mathfrak{R}_{m}({\mathcal{F}}_{d})=\mathbb{E}_{S}[\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{S}({\mathcal{F}}_{d})]
, we have
\mathfrak{R}_{m}({\mathcal{F}}_{d})\leq\frac{\sqrt{d}}{\sqrt{m}}\kappa^{2}
.

Appendix 11.C
Proof of Lemma
5.13

Proof

Let
f_{1}=\sum u_{i}s_{i}\mu_{i}
and
f_{2}=\sum v_{i}s_{i}\mu_{i}
.
Let
{\bm{u}}=[u_{1},u_{2},\cdots]
and
{\bm{v}}=[v_{1},v_{2},\cdots]
.
Then,
{\left\|{\bm{u}}\right\|}_{2}\leq 1
and
{\left\|{\bm{v}}\right\|}_{2}\leq 1
.
For any
S={\left\{x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}\right\}}
, let
{\bm{M}}(x)=[s_{1}\mu_{1}(x),s_{2}\mu_{2}(x)]
and
{\bm{M}}_{j}={\bm{M}}(x_{j})
. Then, we have

\displaystyle\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{S}({\mathcal{F}})

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}}{\left[\sup_{{\left\|{\bm{u}}\right\|}_{2}\leq 1,{\left\|{\bm{v}}\right\|}_{2}\leq 1}{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{j}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{v}}\right|}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}}{\left[{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right\|}_{2}\right]}\leq\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}}{\left[{\left\|\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right\|}_{F}\right]}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}}{\left[\Tr{\left\{{\left(\sum_{j=1}^{n}\sigma_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right)}^{\top}{\left(\sum_{l=1}^{n}\sigma_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}^{\top}\right)}\right\}}^{1/2}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\mathbb{E}_{\sigma_{1},\cdots,\sigma_{n}}{\left[\Tr{\left\{\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}\sigma_{j}\sigma_{l}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}^{\top}\right\}}\right]}}\qquad(\text{Jensen})

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\Tr{\left\{\sum_{j,l=1}^{n}\mathbb{E}{\left[\sigma_{j}\sigma_{l}\right]}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{l}{\bm{M}}_{l}^{\top}\right\}}}=\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{\Tr{\left\{\sum_{j=1}^{n}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{j}{\bm{M}}_{j}^{\top}\right\}}}\leq\frac{1}{n}\sqrt{n\kappa^{4}}.

Here the first line is not equality because of the absolute value. Since this holds for any
S
, we have
\mathfrak{R}_{n}({\mathcal{F}})=\mathbb{E}_{s}[\hat{\mathfrak{R}}_{S}({\mathcal{F}})]\leq\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}
.

By
[
148
, Theorem 4.10]
, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
, both of the following hold simultaneously for any
f\in{\mathcal{F}}
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{n}f(\tilde{x}_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}[f(X)]\right|}\leq\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)};\\
&{\left|\frac{1}{n}\sum_{i=1}^{m}f(x_{i})-\mathbb{E}_{X\sim{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}[f(X)]\right|}\leq\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{m}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)}.\end{aligned}\right.

For any unit vector
{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
, let
f_{\bm{u}}(x)={\bm{u}}^{\top}\Phi(x)
. Then,
{\left\|f_{\bm{u}}\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}=1
, so
f_{\bm{u}}^{2}\in{\mathcal{F}}
.
Moreover, we have

\sum_{i=1}^{m}f_{\bm{u}}(x_{i})^{2}={\left\|{\bm{G}}[{\bm{v}}_{1},\cdots,{\bm{v}}_{d}]{\bm{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}=\sum_{i=1}^{d}\lambda_{i}u_{i}^{2}\geq\lambda_{d}.

Thus, using the above inequalities, assuming that
m\geq n
, we obtain

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}f(\tilde{x}_{j})^{2}\geq\frac{\lambda_{d}}{m}-\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}{\left(4+2\sqrt{2\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)},

which implies the result since
{\left\|{\bm{\Phi}}{\bm{u}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}=\sum_{j=1}^{n}f(\tilde{x}_{j})^{2}
.

Appendix 11.D
Proof of Corollary
5.14

Proof

Denote
F=f^{*}-f_{\Phi}
. Then,
{\bm{y}}-{\bm{y}}_{\Phi}=[F(\tilde{x}_{1}),\cdots,F(\tilde{x}_{n})]
, and
\frac{F^{2}}{{\left\|F\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}}\in{\mathcal{F}}
, where
{\mathcal{F}}
was defined in
Lemma

5.13
.
Therefore, by
Lemma

5.13
, we have

\frac{1}{n}\sum_{j=1}^{n}F(\tilde{x}_{j})\leq\mathbb{E}_{X\sim{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}[F(X)^{2}]+{\left\|F\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}\frac{\kappa^{2}}{\sqrt{n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{2}{\delta}}\right)},

as desired.

Appendix 11.E
Proof of Theorem
5.22

Proof

Let
\mu_{1},\mu_{2},\cdots
be the eigenfunctions of
T_{k}
.
First, let us show that
{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
must be an RKHS.
since
\mu_{1}
is the common top-
1
eigenfunction of
T_{k^{p}}
for all
p\geq 1
, we have
r_{k^{p}}(\mu_{1})\geq r_{k^{p}}(f)
for all
f\in{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
.
By the condition of preserving relative smoothness, this implies that for all
f\in{\mathcal{H}}_{t}\subset{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
, we have
r_{t}(\mu_{1})\geq r_{t}(f)
.
Let
C_{0}=r_{t}(\mu_{1})
. Then, for any
f\in{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
, we have
{\left\|f\right\|}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}\leq\sqrt{C_{0}}{\left\|f\right\|}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{t}}
.
In other words,
{\left\|\cdot\right\|}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{t}}
is a stronger norm than
{\left\|\cdot\right\|}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}
on
{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
.
Thus, for any sequence
(h_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
such that
{\left\|h_{i}-h\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\rightarrow 0
: first, we have
h\in{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
because
{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
is a Hilbert space; second, we have
{\left\|h_{i}-h\right\|}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}\rightarrow 0
.
Similarly, if
{\left\|h_{i}-h^{\prime}\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\rightarrow 0
, then
{\left\|h_{i}-h^{\prime}\right\|}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}\rightarrow 0
.

Consider the inclusion map
I:{\mathcal{H}}_{t}\rightarrow{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
, where
Ih=h
.
For any sequence
(h_{i})\in{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
such that
{\left\|h_{i}-h\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\rightarrow 0
and
{\left\|h_{i}-h^{\prime}\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\rightarrow 0
,
h_{i}
converges to both
h
and
h^{\prime}
under
{\left\|\cdot\right\|}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}
, so we must have
h^{\prime}=h=Ih
.
This means that the graph of
I
is closed, so the closed graph theorem
[
18
, Chapter 2]
guarantees that
I
must be a bounded operator, meaning that there exists a constant
C
such that
{\left\|f\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\leq C{\left\|f\right\|}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{t}}
for all
f\in{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
.

Let
\delta_{x}:f\mapsto f(x)
be the evaluation functional at point
x
.
Since
{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
is an RKHS, there exists a constant
M_{x}>0
such that
|f(x)|\leq M_{x}{\left\|f\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}
for all
f\in{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
.
Thus, for any
f\in{\mathcal{H}}_{t}\subset{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
, we have
|f(x)|\leq M_{x}{\left\|f\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\leq M_{x}C{\left\|f\right\|}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{t}}
. Thus, by
Proposition

5.20
,
{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
is also an RKHS.
Let
{k_{s}}
be the reproducing kernel of
{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
.
From now on, we will use
{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}
to denote
{\mathcal{H}}_{t}
.

Second, we prove by induction that
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{d}
are the top-
d
eigenfunctions of
T_{{k_{s}}}
, and
{\left\langle\mu_{i},\mu_{j}\right\rangle}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}}=0
for any
i\neq j
.
We have already shown that
\mu_{1}
maximizes
r_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}}(f)
over all
f\in{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}
.
Thus,
\mu_{1}
must be the top-
1
eigenfunction of
{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}
.
Suppose
d\geq 2
, and
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{d-1}
are the top-
(d-1)
eigenfunctions and are orthogonal to each other in
{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}
.
Let
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}={\left\{h\;\middle|\;\forall i\in[d-1]:{\left\langle h,\mu_{i}\right\rangle}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}=0\right\}}
.
Obviously,
{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{k^{p}}
is a closed subspace of
{\mathcal{H}}_{k^{p}}
for any
p\geq 1
.
Moreover, for any
f\in{\mathcal{H}}_{0}\cap{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}
and any
i\in[d-1]
, we have
{\left\langle f,\mu_{i}\right\rangle}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}}=s_{i}^{-1}{\left\langle f,\mu_{i}\right\rangle}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}=0
, where
s_{i}
is the eigenvalue of
T_{{k_{s}}}
corresponding to
\mu_{i}
.
Thus,
{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
is a closed subspace of
{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}
.
By the condition of preserving relative smoothness,
\mu_{d}
maximizes
r_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}}(f)
over
f\in{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}\cap{\mathcal{H}}_{0}
. Thus,
\mu_{d}
is the
d
-th eigenfunction of
T_{{k_{s}}}
, and is orthogonal to
\mu_{1},\cdots,\mu_{d-1}
in
{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}
.

Third, we prove by contradiction that
s_{i}\leq M\lambda_{i}
for all
i
.
If this is false, then obviously one can find
t_{1}<t_{2}<\cdots
such that
s_{t_{i}}\geq 1\cdot\lambda_{t_{i}}
for all
i
.
Consider
f=\sum_{i=1}^{\infty}\sqrt{i^{-1}\cdot\lambda_{t_{i}}}\mu_{t_{i}}
.
Then,
{\left\|f\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}=\sum_{i}i^{-1}=+\infty
.
Since
{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}\subset{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
, this implies that
{\left\|f\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}}^{2}=+\infty=\sum_{i}\frac{\lambda_{t_{i}}}{i\cdot s_{t_{i}}}\leq\sum_{i}\frac{1}{i^{2}}<+\infty
, which is a contradiction.

Fourth, we find a function
s(\lambda)
that satisfies the conditions in the theorem to interpolate
(\lambda_{i},s_{i})
for all
i
.
We first point out that we can without loss of generality assume that
\lambda_{i}<2\lambda_{i+1}
for all
i
:
If there is an
i
that does not satisfy this condition, we simply insert some new
\lambda
’s between
\lambda_{i}
and
\lambda_{i+1}
,
whose corresponding
s
’s are the linear interpolations between
s_{i}
and
s_{i+1}
, so that
s_{i}\leq M\lambda_{i}
still holds.
With this assumption, it suffices to construct a series of bump functions
\{f_{i}\}_{i=1}^{\infty}
,
where
f_{i}\equiv 0
if
\lambda_{i}=\lambda_{i+1}
;
otherwise,
f_{i}(\lambda)=s_{i}-s_{i+1}
for
\lambda\geq\lambda_{i}
and
f_{i}(\lambda)=0
for
\lambda\leq\lambda_{i+1}
.
Such bump functions are
C^{\infty}
and monotonically non-decreasing.
Then, define
s(\lambda)=\sum_{i}f_{i}(\lambda)
for
\lambda>0
, and
s(0)=0
.
This sum of bump functions converges everywhere on
(0,+\infty)
, since it is a finite sum locally everywhere.
Clearly this
s
is monotonic, interpolates all the points, continuous on
[0,+\infty)
and
C^{\infty}
on
(0,+\infty)
.
And for all
\lambda
that is not
\lambda_{i}
, for instance
\lambda\in(\lambda_{i+1},\lambda_{i})
,
there is
s(\lambda)\leq s(\lambda_{i})\leq M\lambda_{i}\leq 2M\lambda_{i+1}\leq 2M\lambda
.
Thus,
s(\lambda)=O(\lambda)
for
\lambda\in[0,+\infty)
.

Remark 11.4
.

In general, we cannot guarantee that
s(\lambda)
is differentiable at
\lambda=0
.
Here is a counterexample:
\lambda_{i}=3^{-i}
, and
s_{i}=3^{-i}
if
i
is odd and
2\cdot 3^{-i}
if
i
is even.
Were
s(\lambda)
to be differentiable at
\lambda=0
, its derivative would be
1
and also would be
2
, a contradiction.

Appendix 11.F
Proof of Theorem
5.25

Proof

Let
\hat{\lambda}_{1}\geq\cdots\geq\hat{\lambda}_{m+n}
be the eigenvalues of
\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}
.
It is easy to show that
{\bm{Q}}
has the same eigenvectors as
\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}
, with eigenvalues
g(\hat{\lambda}_{1}),\cdots,g(\hat{\lambda}_{2})
.
By
Lemma

5.29
and Borel-Cantelli lemma, as
n\rightarrow\infty
,
\hat{\lambda}_{1}\xrightarrow{\textit{a.s.}}\lambda_{1}
.
For simplicity, let us assume that
\lambda
is slightly larger than
\lambda_{1}
, so almost surely there is
\hat{\lambda}_{1}\leq\lambda
.
Then, all eigenvalues of
{\bm{Q}}
are in
[\rho_{\min},\rho_{\max}]
.

The first part of this proof is to bound
{\left\|{\bm{u}}_{t}\right\|}_{2}
, where
{\bm{u}}_{t}:=(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})
.
Let
{\bm{\theta}}_{t}
be the
{\bm{\theta}}
at iteration
t
, and
{\bm{\theta}}_{*}
be the optimal solution. Since
{\bm{\theta}}_{0}={\bm{0}}
, we have

\displaystyle{\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t}=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\left[{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\left[(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}\right]}\right)}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}+\gamma\tilde{{\bm{y}}}\right]}

(41)

\displaystyle-{\left[{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\left[(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}\right]}\right)}{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}+\gamma\tilde{{\bm{y}}}\right]}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\left[(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}\right]}\right)}{\left({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t-1}\right)}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle{\displaystyle\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\left[(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}\right]}\right)}^{t}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}.

Note that

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\left[(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}\right]}\right)}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\left[(m+n){\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}\right]}\right)}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}.

Thus, by propagating
{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}
from left to right, we get

{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})={\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right)}^{t}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*},

where
{\bm{R}}:=(m+n){\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}
is a
p.s.d.
matrix.
Denote the smallest and largest eigenvalues of
{\bm{R}}
by
\tilde{\lambda}_{\min}
and
\tilde{\lambda}_{\max}
.
Then,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\min}\geq n\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}
.
In terms of
\tilde{\lambda}_{\max}
, we have

(m+n){\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}={\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{\frac{r-1}{2}}{\left({\bm{G}}_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\right)}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{\frac{r-1}{2}}.

By Sylvester’s theorem, all non-zero eigenvalues of
{\bm{G}}_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{\frac{1}{2}}
are the eigenvalues of
\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\bm{G}}_{k}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}
,

i.e.
the non-zero eigenvalues of
{\bm{G}}_{K,n}
.
By
Lemma

5.29
,
\frac{1}{n}{\left\|{\bm{G}}_{K,n}\right\|}_{2}\xrightarrow{\textit{a.s.}}\lambda_{1}
,
so suppose
{\left\|{\bm{G}}_{K,n}\right\|}_{2}\leq n\lambda
.
Then,
\tilde{\lambda}_{\max}\leq n\lambda^{r}+n\beta_{n}\rho_{\max}
.

Since
{\bm{M}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}=\tilde{{\bm{y}}}
, and
{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\bm{M}}={\bm{R}}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}
, we have
{\bm{R}}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}={\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}\tilde{{\bm{y}}}
.
Note that
{\bm{R}}({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}})=({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}){\bm{R}}
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})

\displaystyle={\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right)}^{t}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}

\displaystyle={\bm{R}}^{-1}({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}})^{t}{\bm{R}}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}

\displaystyle={\bm{R}}^{-1}({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}})^{t}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}\tilde{{\bm{y}}}.

Now we bound
\|{\bm{u}}_{t}\|_{2}
.
First, note that for any matrices
{\bm{A}},{\bm{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
where
{\bm{B}}
is
p.s.d.
,
there is
{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{B}}{\bm{A}}{\bm{u}}\leq\|{\bm{B}}\|_{2}\|{\bm{A}}{\bm{u}}\|_{2}^{2}\leq\|{\bm{B}}\|_{2}\|{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}\|_{2}\|{\bm{u}}\|_{2}^{2}
for any
{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
,
so
\|{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{B}}{\bm{A}}\|_{2}\leq\|{\bm{B}}\|_{2}\|{\bm{A}}^{\top}{\bm{A}}\|_{2}
.
Second, note that the last
m
elements of
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}
are zeros,
which means that
\tilde{{\bm{y}}}=\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}\tilde{{\bm{y}}}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\|{\bm{u}}_{t}\|_{2}

\displaystyle={\left\|(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle={\left\|(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\bm{R}}^{-1}{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right)}^{t}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}\tilde{{\bm{y}}}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle={\left\|(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right)}^{t/2}{\bm{R}}^{-1}{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right)}^{t/2}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r/2}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}\tilde{{\bm{y}}}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\left\|{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right)}^{t/2}{\bm{R}}^{-1}{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right)}^{t/2}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\tilde{{\bm{y}}}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\lambda}_{\min}}{\left\|{\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right\|}_{2}^{t}(n\lambda_{1}^{r})\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2},

where the last step is because we have already proved
{\left\|(m+n)\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{r}\tilde{{\bm{I}}}_{n}\right\|}_{2}\leq n\lambda_{1}^{r}
.

Now, for
\gamma=\frac{1}{n\lambda^{r}}
, when
n
is sufficiently large it is less than
\frac{2}{\tilde{\lambda}_{\max}+\tilde{\lambda}_{\min}}
, because
\beta_{n}=o(1)
.
Thus,
{\left\|{\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma{\bm{R}}\right\|}_{2}\leq 1-\frac{\tilde{\lambda}_{\min}}{n\lambda^{r}}\leq 1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}
.
Thus, we have

\|{\bm{u}}_{t}\|_{2}\leq{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}^{t}\frac{\lambda^{r}}{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}.

The second part of this proof is to bound
\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\|_{2}
.
Let us return to
Eqn.

41
, which says that

\displaystyle{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle={\left\|{\left({\bm{I}}_{m+n}-\gamma n\beta_{n}{\bm{Q}}\right)}{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})-\gamma{\bm{Q}}{\bm{u}}_{t}\right\|}_{2}

\displaystyle\leq{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\|}_{2}+\frac{\rho_{\max}}{n\lambda^{r}}\|{\bm{u}}_{t}\|_{2}.

Here again, we assume that
n
is large enough so that
\lambda^{r}>\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}
. This implies that

\displaystyle{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t+1})\right\|}_{2}-t{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}^{t}\frac{\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}{\left[{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\|}_{2}-(t-1){\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}^{t-1}\frac{\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}\right]}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\cdots\leq{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}^{t}{\left[{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}+\frac{\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}\right]}.

Thus, there is
{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}^{t}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}+t{\left(1-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}^{t-1}\frac{\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}
.
Using
1-x\leq e^{-x}
, we have

{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\|}_{2}\leq\exp{\left(-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}t}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}+t\exp{\left(-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}(t-1)}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}\frac{\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}.

When
t=t_{0}:=\frac{4\lambda^{r}}{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}\log\frac{2\lambda^{r}\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}}
, by
\log(2x)\leq x
for
x>0
, we have

\exp{\left(\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}\frac{t}{2}\right)}\geq{\left(\frac{2\lambda^{r}\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}}\right)}^{2}\geq\frac{4\lambda^{r}\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}}\log{\left(\frac{2\lambda^{r}\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}^{2}\rho_{\min}^{2}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}}\right)}.

Let
F(t):=\exp{\left(\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{2\lambda^{r}}t\right)}-\frac{\rho_{\max}\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}}t
. Then we have
F(t_{0})\geq 0
.
And it is easy to show that for all
t\geq\frac{t_{0}}{2}
, there is
F^{\prime}(t)\geq 0
.
This means that when
t\geq t_{0}
, there is
F(t)\geq 0
, so we have

{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\|}_{2}\leq\exp{\left(-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}t}{\lambda^{r}}\right)}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}+\exp{\left(-\frac{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}{\lambda^{r}}{\left(\frac{t}{2}-1\right)}\right)}\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}.

Hence, when
t\geq\max{\left\{\frac{2\lambda^{r}}{\beta_{n}\rho_{\min}}\log\frac{2}{\epsilon}+2,t_{0}\right\}}
, we have
{\left\|{\bm{Q}}({\bm{\theta}}_{*}-{\bm{\theta}}_{t})\right\|}_{2}\leq\epsilon\|{\bm{Q}}{\bm{\theta}}_{*}\|_{2}
, which implies that the relative estimation error of
\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}
is less than
\epsilon
.

Appendix 11.G
Proof of Theorem
5.26

Proof

Let us look at the three conditions used in
Theorem

5.12
.

•

Eigenvalue decay (EVD):
This is a condition of the theorem.

•

Embedding condition (EMB):
For any
f=\sum u_{i}\mu_{i}\in{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}
, for
{P_{\mathcal{X}}}
-almost all
x
we have
f(x)^{2}={\left(\sum u_{i}\mu_{i}(x)\right)}^{2}\leq{\left(\sum\frac{u_{i}^{2}}{\lambda_{i}}\right)}{\left(\sum\lambda_{i}\mu_{i}(x)^{2}\right)}\leq{\left\|f\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}\kappa^{2}\leq\kappa^{2}M{\left\|f\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}}^{2}
. Thus, EMB holds with
c_{2}=\kappa\sqrt{M}
.

•

Source condition (SRC):
This holds with
c_{3}=\sqrt{\epsilon}{\left\|f^{*}\right\|}_{{P_{\mathcal{X}}}}
.

Thus, the theorem can be proved using the result in
[
41
]
.

Appendix 11.H
Proof of Theorem
5.28

Proposition 11.5
.

For any p.s.d. matrices
{\bm{A}},{\bm{B}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d\times d}
, we have
\Tr({\bm{A}}{\bm{B}})\leq{\left\|{\bm{A}}\right\|}_{2}\Tr({\bm{B}})
.

Proof

An elementary proof can be found at
https://math.stackexchange.com/questions/2241879/reference-for-trace-norm-inequality
.

Lemma 11.6
.

For any
\delta\in(0,1)
, with probability at least
1-\delta
the following holds for all
p\geq 1
:

{\left|{\hat{k}^{p}}(x,x_{j})-k^{p}(x,x_{j})\right|}\leq{\left(p-1\right)}\lambda_{\max}^{p-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\quad\text{for all }j\in[m+n],x\in{\mathcal{X}},

which implies that

{\left|{\hat{k}_{s}}(x,x_{j})-{k_{s}}(x,x_{j})\right|}\leq\left.\nabla_{\lambda}{\left(\frac{s(\lambda)}{\lambda}\right)}\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}.

Proof

For any
x^{\prime}\in{\mathcal{X}}
and any
p\geq 1
, we have

{\left\|k^{p}(\cdot,x^{\prime})\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}={\left\|\sum_{i}\lambda_{i}^{p}\mu_{i}(x^{\prime})\mu_{i}(\cdot)\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}=\sum_{i}\frac{\lambda_{i}^{2p}\mu_{i}(x^{\prime})^{2}}{\lambda_{i}}\leq\lambda_{1}^{2p-2}\kappa^{2}.

Let
F_{p}(x)={\bm{u}}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x)
, where
{\bm{u}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+n}
satisfies
{\left\|{\bm{u}}\right\|}_{1}\leq 1
.
Since
{\left\langle k(x_{i},\cdot),k(x_{j},\cdot)\right\rangle}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}=k(x_{i},x_{j})
, we have
{\left\langle{\bm{v}}_{k},{\bm{v}}_{k}\right\rangle}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}={\bm{G}}_{k}
. Thus,

{\left\|F_{p}\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}={\left\langle{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p}{\bm{v}}_{k},{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p}{\bm{v}}_{k}\right\rangle}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}={\bm{u}}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{2p+1}}{(m+n)^{2p}}{\bm{u}}.

Since
{\bm{G}}_{k}
is
p.s.d.
, we can define
{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}
.
Using
Proposition

11.5
, we have

\displaystyle{\left\|F_{p}\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}^{2}

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{2p+1}}{(m+n)^{2p}}{\bm{u}}=\Tr{\left\{{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{2p}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}\right\}}

\displaystyle=\Tr{\left\{{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{2p}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}\right\}}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{2p}\Tr{\left({\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}\right)}.

Moreover,
\Tr{\left({\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}\right)}={\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}{\bm{u}}=\sum_{i,j=1}^{m+n}u_{i}u_{j}k(x_{i},x_{j})\leq\sum_{i,j=1}^{m+n}{\left|u_{i}u_{j}k(x_{i},x_{j})\right|}\leq\kappa^{2}{\left\|{\bm{u}}\right\|}_{1}^{2}\leq\kappa^{2}
. Thus, we have
{\left\|F_{p}\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{p}\kappa
for all
p\geq 1
.

For any
p\geq 1
, define
{\bm{v}}_{k^{p}}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{m+n}
as
{\bm{v}}_{k^{p}}(x)[i]=k^{p}(x,x_{i})
for
i\in[m+n]
. Then,

\displaystyle{\left|k^{p}(x,x_{j})-{\hat{k}^{p}}(x,x_{j})\right|}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle{\left|k^{p}(x,x_{j})-\frac{1}{(m+n)^{p-1}}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x)^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-2}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle{\left|k^{p}(x,x_{j})-\frac{1}{m+n}{\bm{v}}_{k^{p-1}}(x)^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})\right|}

\displaystyle+\sum_{q=1}^{p-2}\frac{1}{(m+n)^{q}}{\left|{\bm{v}}_{k^{p-q}}(x)^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{q-1}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})-{\bm{v}}_{k^{p-q-1}}(x)^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{q}}{m+n}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})\right|}.

Since
f(z)=k^{p-1}(x,z)k(x_{j},z)\in{\mathcal{F}}
, where
{\mathcal{F}}
was defined in
Lemma

5.13
,
the first term can be bounded as

\displaystyle{\left|k^{p}(x,x_{j})-\frac{1}{m+n}{\bm{v}}_{k^{p-1}}(x)^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})\right|}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle{\left|\int k^{p-1}(x,z)k(x_{j},z)d{P_{\mathcal{X}}}(z)-\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{i=1}^{m+n}k^{p-1}(x,x_{i})k(x_{j},x_{i})\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}^{p-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)},

where the last step uses
{\left\|k^{p-1}(x,\cdot)\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\leq\lambda_{1}^{p-2}\kappa
, and
{\left\|k(x_{j},\cdot)\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\leq\kappa
.

For the second term, since
{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})={\bm{G}}_{k}{\bm{e}}_{j}
where
{\bm{e}}_{j}=[0,\cdots,0,1,0,\cdots,0]
, we have

\displaystyle\sum_{q=1}^{p-2}\frac{1}{(m+n)^{q}}{\left|{\bm{v}}_{k^{p-q}}(x)^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{q-1}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})-{\bm{v}}_{k^{p-q-1}}(x)^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{q}}{m+n}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})\right|}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle{\left|\int k^{p-q-1}(x,z){\left[{\bm{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{q}{\bm{v}}_{k}(z)\right]}d{P_{\mathcal{X}}}(z)-\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{j=1}^{m+n}k^{p-q-1}(x,x_{j}){\left[{\bm{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{q}{\bm{v}}_{k}(x_{j})\right]}\right|}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\lambda_{1}^{p-q-2}\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{q}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)},

where the last step uses
{\left\|k^{p-q-1}(x,\cdot)\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\leq\lambda_{1}^{p-q-2}\kappa
, and
{\left\|{\bm{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{q}{\bm{v}}_{k}\right\|}_{{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{q}\kappa
since
{\left\|{\bm{e}}_{j}\right\|}_{1}=1
.
Finally, note that
\nabla_{\lambda}{\left(\frac{s(\lambda)}{\lambda}\right)}=\sum_{p=1}^{\infty}\pi_{p}(p-1)\lambda^{p-2}
. Combining all of the above yields the result.

Corollary 11.7
.

Under the settings of
Lemma

11.6
, we have

\displaystyle\left|{k_{s^{2}}}(x_{i},x_{j})-\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{k_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|+\left|\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}-\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{k_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 2s(\lambda_{\max})\left.\nabla_{\lambda}{\left(\frac{s(\lambda)}{\lambda}\right)}\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}

holds for all
i,j\in[m+n]
, where
\lambda_{\max}=\max{\left\{\lambda_{1},\hat{\lambda}_{1}\right\}}
.

Proof

Let
F_{p,q}(x)={\bm{u}}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p}{\bm{v}}_{k^{q}}(x)

for any
\|{\bm{u}}\|_{1}\leq 1
and any
p\geq 0,q\geq 1
.
By
Proposition

11.5
, we have

\displaystyle\|F_{p,q}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}^{2}

\displaystyle={\bm{u}}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p}{\bm{G}}_{k^{2q-1}}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p}{\bm{u}}

\displaystyle=\Tr{\left({\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p-1/2}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k^{2q-1}}}{m+n}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p-1/2}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{2p-1}{\left\|\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k^{2q-1}}}{m+n}\right\|}_{2}\Tr{\left({\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}{\bm{u}}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}^{1/2}\right)}

\displaystyle=\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{2p-1}{\left\|\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k^{2q-1}}}{m+n}\right\|}_{2}{\bm{u}}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k}{\bm{u}}\leq\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{2p-1}{\left\|\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k^{2q-1}}}{m+n}\right\|}_{2}\kappa^{2}.

For any unit vector
{\bm{w}}\in\mathbb{R}^{m+n}
, we have

\hat{\lambda}_{1}\geq{\bm{w}}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}{\bm{w}}=\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{i,j=1}^{m+n}w_{i}w_{j}K(x_{i},x_{j})=\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{t}\lambda_{t}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{t}{\bm{w}},

where
{\bm{M}}_{t}\in\mathbb{R}^{(m+n)\times(m+n)}
is defined as
{\bm{M}}_{t}[i,j]=\mu_{t}(x_{i})\mu_{t}(x_{j})
.
Thus, we have

{\bm{w}}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k^{2q-1}}}{m+n}{\bm{w}}=\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{t}\lambda_{t}^{2q-1}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{t}{\bm{w}}\leq\lambda_{1}^{2q-2}\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{t}\lambda_{t}{\bm{w}}^{\top}{\bm{M}}_{t}{\bm{w}}\leq\lambda_{1}^{2q-2}\hat{\lambda}_{1},

which implies that
{\left\|\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k^{2q-1}}}{m+n}\right\|}_{2}\leq\lambda_{1}^{2q-2}\hat{\lambda}_{1}
. Thus,
\|F_{p,q}\|_{{\mathcal{H}}_{k}}^{2}\leq\lambda_{1}^{2q-2}\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{2p}\kappa^{2}
.

Note that
\langle{\bm{v}}_{k},{\bm{v}}_{k}\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}={\bm{G}}_{k^{2}}
. So for any
p,q\geq 1
and any
i,j\in[m+n]
, there is:

\displaystyle\left|k^{p+q}(x_{i},x_{j})-{\left\langle\hat{k}^{p}(x_{i},\cdot),k^{q}(x_{j},\cdot)\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|=\left|{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{k^{p+q}}{\bm{e}}_{j}-{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-1}}{(m+n)^{p-1}}{\bm{G}}_{k^{q+1}}{\bm{e}}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{p-1}\left|{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-t}}{(m+n)^{p-t}}{\bm{G}}_{k^{q+t}}{\bm{e}}_{j}-{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-t-1}}{(m+n)^{p-t-1}}{\bm{G}}_{k^{q+t+1}}{\bm{e}}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{p-1}\left|\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{l=1}^{m+n}{\left[{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p-t-1}{\bm{v}}_{k}\right]}(x_{l}){\left[{\bm{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{k^{q+t}}\right]}(x_{l})\right.

\displaystyle\left.-{\left\langle{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p-t-1}{\bm{v}}_{k},{\bm{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{k^{q+t}}\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{p-1}\lambda_{1}^{q+t-1}\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{p-t-1}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\leq(p-1)\lambda_{\max}^{p+q-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}.

Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left|{k_{s^{2}}}(x_{i},x_{j})-\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{k_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}\left|\pi_{p}\pi_{q}{\left(k^{p+q}(x_{i},x_{j})-{\left\langle\hat{k}^{p}(x_{i},\cdot),k^{q}(x_{j},\cdot)\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}\pi_{p}\pi_{q}(p-1)\lambda_{\max}^{p+q-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}.

Similarly, we can show that:

\displaystyle\left|{\left\langle\hat{k}^{p}(x_{i},\cdot),\hat{k}^{q}(x_{j},\cdot)\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}-{\left\langle\hat{k}^{p}(x_{i},\cdot),k^{q}(x_{j},\cdot)\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\left|{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-1}}{(m+n)^{p-1}}{\bm{G}}_{k^{2}}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{q-1}}{(m+n)^{q-1}}{\bm{e}}_{j}-{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-1}}{(m+n)^{p-1}}{\bm{G}}_{k^{q+1}}{\bm{e}}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{q-1}\left|{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-1}}{(m+n)^{p-1}}{\bm{G}}_{k^{t+1}}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{q-t}}{(m+n)^{q-t}}{\bm{e}}_{j}-{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{p-1}}{(m+n)^{p-1}}{\bm{G}}_{k^{t+2}}\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}^{q-t-1}}{(m+n)^{q-t-1}}{\bm{e}}_{j}\right|

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{q-1}\left|\frac{1}{m+n}\sum_{l=1}^{m+n}{\left[{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p-1}{\bm{v}}_{k^{t+1}}\right]}(x_{l}){\left[{\bm{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{q-t-1}{\bm{v}}_{k}\right]}(x_{l})\right.

\displaystyle\left.-{\left\langle{\bm{e}}_{i}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{p-1}{\bm{v}}_{k^{t+1}},{\bm{e}}_{j}^{\top}{\left(\frac{{\bm{G}}_{k}}{m+n}\right)}^{q-t-1}{\bm{v}}_{k}\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{t=1}^{q-1}\lambda_{1}^{t}\hat{\lambda}_{1}^{p+q-t-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\leq(q-1)\lambda_{\max}^{p+q-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)},

which implies that

\displaystyle\left|\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}-\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{k_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}\left|\pi_{p}\pi_{q}{\left({\left\langle\hat{k}^{p}(x_{i},\cdot),\hat{k}^{q}(x_{j},\cdot)\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}-{\left\langle\hat{k}^{p}(x_{i},\cdot),k^{q}(x_{j},\cdot)\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right)}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}\pi_{p}\pi_{q}(q-1)\lambda_{\max}^{p+q-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}.

Combining the above inequalities, we obtain

\displaystyle\left|{k_{s^{2}}}(x_{i},x_{j})-\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{k_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|+\left|\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}-\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{k_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\sum_{p,q=1}^{\infty}\pi_{p}\pi_{q}(p+q-2)\lambda_{\max}^{p+q-2}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}

\displaystyle=\;

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}\left.\nabla_{\lambda}{\left(\frac{s(\lambda)^{2}}{\lambda^{2}}\right)}\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)},

so we get the result by expanding the derivative.

We now prove
Theorem

5.28
.

Proof

Define
{\bm{v}}_{{k_{s}},n}(x)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
such that
{\bm{v}}_{{k_{s}},n}(x)[i]={k_{s}}(x,x_{i})
.
Define
{\bm{v}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}(x)
similarly.
Recall the formulas
\tilde{f}=\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{{k_{s}},n}
and
\hat{f}=\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}
.
Define
f^{\dagger}:=\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{\top}{\bm{v}}_{{k_{s}},n}
.
Since
{\bm{G}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}
is
p.s.d.
, we can see that
\|\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{2}\leq\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}}{n\beta_{n}}
,
and
\|\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{1}\leq\sqrt{n}\|\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}\|_{2}
.
So by
Corollary

11.7
, we have

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{f}-f^{\dagger}\right\|}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}^{2}

\displaystyle=\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{\top}{\left\langle{\bm{v}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}-{\bm{v}}_{{k_{s}},n},{\bm{v}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}-{\bm{v}}_{{k_{s}},n}\right\rangle}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}

\displaystyle=\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{\top}{\left(\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}+{k_{s^{2}}}(x_{i},x_{j})-2\langle{\hat{k}_{s}}(x_{i},\cdot),{k_{s}}(x_{j},\cdot)\rangle_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}\right)}\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}

\displaystyle\leq 2s(\lambda_{\max})\left.\nabla_{\lambda}{\left(\frac{s(\lambda)}{\lambda}\right)}\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\beta_{n}^{-2}\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}.

By the definitions of
\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}
and
\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}
, we can also see that:

{\left({\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}+n\beta_{n}{\bm{I}}_{n}\right)}{\left(\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)}={\left({\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}-{\bm{G}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}\right)}\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}.

(42)

Note that
{\left\|{\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}-{\bm{G}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}\right\|}_{2}\leq n{\left\|{\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}-{\bm{G}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}\right\|}_{\max}
.
Here
{\left\|{\bm{M}}\right\|}_{\max}=\max{\left|{\bm{M}}[i,j]\right|}
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle{\left\|\tilde{f}-f^{\dagger}\right\|}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}^{2}

\displaystyle={\left(\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)}^{\top}{\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}{\left(\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)}

\displaystyle={\left(\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)}^{\top}{\left({\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}-{\bm{G}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}\right)}\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-n\beta_{n}{\left(\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)}^{\top}{\left(\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq{\left\|\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|{\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}-{\bm{G}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|{\bm{G}}_{{k_{s}},n}-{\bm{G}}_{{\hat{k}_{s}},n}\right\|}_{2}{\left\|\hat{{\bm{\alpha}}}\right\|}_{2}-0

\displaystyle\leq 2\left.\nabla_{\lambda}{\left(\frac{s(\lambda)}{\lambda}\right)}\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\beta_{n}^{-2}\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n}.

And note that we have
{\left\|\tilde{f}-f^{\dagger}\right\|}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}^{2}\leq s(\lambda_{1}){\left\|\tilde{f}-f^{\dagger}\right\|}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}^{2}\leq s(\lambda_{\max}){\left\|\tilde{f}-f^{\dagger}\right\|}_{{\mathcal{H}}_{{k_{s}}}}^{2}
. Thus,

\displaystyle{\left\|\hat{f}-\tilde{f}\right\|}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq 2{\left({\left\|\hat{f}-f^{\dagger}\right\|}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}^{2}+{\left\|\tilde{f}-f^{\dagger}\right\|}_{P_{\mathcal{X}}}^{2}\right)}

\displaystyle\leq 8s(\lambda_{\max})\left.\nabla_{\lambda}{\left(\frac{s(\lambda)}{\lambda}\right)}\right|_{\lambda=\lambda_{\max}}\frac{\beta_{n}^{-2}\kappa^{4}}{\sqrt{m+n}}{\left(2+\sqrt{2\log\frac{1}{\delta}}\right)}\frac{\|{\bm{y}}\|_{2}^{2}}{n},

as desired.

Chapter 12
Proofs for Chapter 6

Appendix 12.A
Proof of Theorem
6.4

We need the following classical result in convex optimization.

Theorem 12.1

(
[
141
]
, p. 16)
.

Let
f
be a convex and
L
-smooth function (
Definition

6.9
) on
{\mathcal{D}}\subseteq\mathbb{R}^{d_{{\mathcal{X}}}}
.
Suppose it has a unique finite minimizer
x^{*}
.
If one minimizes
f
with gradient descent
x_{t+1}=x_{t}-\eta\nabla f(x_{t})
, staring from
x_{0}
with a fixed learning rate
\eta\leq\frac{1}{L}
, then we have

f(x_{T})\leq f(x^{*})+\frac{1}{\eta T}{\left\|x_{0}-x^{*}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }T>0.

Now let us prove
Theorem

6.4
.

Proof

Static GRW.
We first prove the result for static GRW where
q_{i}^{(t)}=q_{i}>0
for all
t
. Let
q^{*}=\min_{i}q_{i}
. The minimization objective is
F(\theta)=\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}(x_{i}^{\top}\theta-y_{i})^{2}
, whose Hessian is
\nabla_{\theta}^{2}F(\theta)=2\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}x_{i}x_{i}^{\top}
. Let
A=\sum_{i=1}^{n}{\left\|x_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{2}
.
Since
q_{i}\in[0,1]
, for any unit vector
{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{{\mathcal{X}}}}
, we have

{\bm{v}}^{\top}\nabla_{\theta}^{2}F(\theta){\bm{v}}=2\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}(x_{i}^{\top}{\bm{v}})^{2}\leq 2\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}{\left\|x_{i}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\leq 2A,

which by
Definition

6.9
implies that
F
is
2A
-smooth. Thus, we have

F(\theta_{2})\leq F(\theta_{1})+{\left\langle\nabla_{\theta}F(\theta_{1}),\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}\right\rangle}+A{\left\|\theta_{2}-\theta_{1}\right\|}_{2}^{2}\qquad\text{for all }\theta_{1},\theta_{2}\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{{\mathcal{X}}}}.

(43)

Denote
g(\theta^{(t)})={\bm{X}}^{\top}\theta^{(t)}-{\bm{Y}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
.
Let
\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}={\textnormal{diag}}{\left(\sqrt{q_{1}},\cdots,\sqrt{q_{n}}\right)}
.
Then, we have
F(\theta^{(t)})={\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|}_{2}^{2}
,
which implies that
\nabla F(\theta^{(t)})=2{\bm{X}}{\bm{Q}}g(\theta^{(t)})
.
The update rule of static GRW with gradient descent is thus given by

\theta^{(t+1)}=\theta^{(t)}-\eta\sum_{i=1}^{n}q_{i}x_{i}{\left(f^{(t)}(x_{i})-y_{i}\right)}=\theta^{(t)}-\eta{\bm{X}}{\bm{Q}}g(\theta^{(t)}).

By
Equation

43
, we have

F(\theta^{(t+1)})\leq F(\theta^{(t)})-2\eta g(\theta^{(t)})^{\top}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}{\bm{Q}}g(\theta^{(t)})+A{\left\|\eta{\bm{X}}{\bm{Q}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|}_{2}^{2}.

Since
x_{1},\cdots,x_{n}
are linearly independent,
{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}
is a positive definite matrix. Let its smallest eigenvalue be
\lambda_{\min}>0
.
Note that
{\left\|{\bm{Q}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|}_{2}\geq\sqrt{q^{*}}{\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|}_{2}=\sqrt{q^{*}F(\theta^{(t)})}
. Thus, we have
g(\theta^{(t)})^{\top}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}{\bm{Q}}g(\theta^{(t)})\geq q^{*}\lambda_{\min}F(\theta^{(t)})
. So we have

\displaystyle F(\theta^{(t+1)})

\displaystyle\leq F(\theta^{(t)})-2\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}F(\theta^{(t)})+A\eta^{2}{\left\|{\bm{X}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|}_{2}^{2}{\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|}_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq F(\theta^{(t)})-2\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}F(\theta^{(t)})+A\eta^{2}{\left\|{\bm{X}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|}_{F}^{2}F(\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq F(\theta^{(t)})-2\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}F(\theta^{(t)})+A\eta^{2}{\left\|{\bm{X}}\right\|}_{F}^{2}F(\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle={\left(1-2\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}+A^{2}\eta^{2}\right)}F(\theta^{(t)}).

Let
\eta_{0}=\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{A^{2}}
.
For any
\eta\leq\eta_{0}
, we have
F(\theta^{(t+1)})\leq(1-\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min})F(\theta^{(t)})
for all
t
, which implies that
F(\theta^{(t)})
must converge to zero.
Since every
q_{i}>0
, this implies that the ERM risk must converge to zero.

Dynamic GRW.
By
Assumption

6.3
, for any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
t_{\epsilon}
such that for all
t\geq t_{\epsilon}
, we have
q_{i}^{(t)}\in(q_{i}-\epsilon,q_{i}+\epsilon)
for all
i
. Let
\lambda_{\max}
and
\lambda_{\min}
be the largest and smallest eigenvalues of
{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}
, where
\lambda_{\min}>0
.
Fix
\epsilon=\min{\left\{\frac{q^{*}}{3},\frac{(q^{*}\lambda_{\min})^{2}}{12\lambda_{\max}^{2}}\right\}}
. Then,
t_{\epsilon}
is also fixed.

Denote
{\bm{Q}}={\textnormal{diag}}{\left(q_{1},\cdots,q_{n}\right)}
. When
t\geq t_{\epsilon}
, the update rule of dynamic GRW is
\theta^{(t+1)}=\theta^{(t)}-\eta{\bm{X}}{\bm{Q}}_{\epsilon}^{(t)}{\left({\bm{X}}^{\top}\theta^{(t)}-{\bm{Y}}\right)}
. We use the subscript
\epsilon
to indicate that
{\left\|{\bm{Q}}_{\epsilon}^{(t)}-{\bm{Q}}\right\|}_{2}<\epsilon
.
Because
q_{i}+\epsilon\sqrt{(q_{i}+3\epsilon)q_{i}}
and
q_{i}-\epsilon\geq\sqrt{(q_{i}-\epsilon)q_{i}}
for all
\epsilon\leq\frac{q_{i}}{3}
, we can rewrite
{\bm{Q}}_{\epsilon}^{(t)}
as
{\bm{Q}}_{\epsilon}^{(t)}=\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}
.
So by
Equation

43
, we have

F(\theta^{(t+1)})\leq F(\theta^{(t)})-2\eta g(\theta^{(t)})^{\top}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})+A{\left\|\eta{\bm{X}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|}_{2}^{2}.

For all
\epsilon<\frac{q_{i}}{3}
, we have
\sqrt{q_{i}+3\epsilon}-\sqrt{q_{i}}\leq\sqrt{3\epsilon}
and
\sqrt{q_{i}}-\sqrt{q_{i}-3\epsilon}\leq\sqrt{3\epsilon}
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left|g(\theta^{(t)})^{\top}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}\left(\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}-\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right)\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right|

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}\left(\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}-\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right)\right\|_{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}-\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\lambda_{\max}\sqrt{3\epsilon}F(\theta^{(t)}).

Since
g(\theta^{(t)})^{\top}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}{\bm{Q}}g(\theta^{(t)})\geq q^{*}\lambda_{\min}F(\theta^{(t)})
, and
\epsilon\leq\frac{(q^{*}\lambda_{\min})^{2}}{12\lambda_{\max}^{2}}
, we have

g(\theta^{(t)})^{\top}{\bm{Q}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}^{\top}{\bm{X}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\geq\left(q^{*}\lambda_{\min}-\lambda_{\max}\sqrt{3\epsilon}\right)F(\theta^{(t)})\geq\frac{1}{2}q^{*}\lambda_{\min}F(\theta^{(t)}).

Thus, for all
\epsilon\leq\frac{1}{3}
, we have

\displaystyle F(\theta^{(t+1)})

\displaystyle\leq F(\theta^{(t)})-\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}F(\theta^{(t)})+A\eta^{2}\left\|{\bm{X}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}^{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1-\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}+A^{2}\eta^{2}(1+3\epsilon))F(\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle\leq(1-\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}+2A^{2}\eta^{2})F(\theta^{(t)})

Let
\eta_{0}=\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{4A^{2}}
. For any
\eta\leq\eta_{0}
, we have
F(\theta^{(t+1)})\leq(1-\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}/2)F(\theta^{(t)})
for all
t\geq t_{\epsilon}
, which implies that
\lim_{t\rightarrow\infty}F(\theta^{(t)})=0
. Thus, the ERM risk converges to 0.

Appendix 12.B
Proof of Theorem
6.6

The proof of this theorem is largely based on the following result.

Lemma 12.2

(Approximation Theorem)
.

For a wide NN
f^{(t)}
trained by any GRW satisfying
Assumption

6.3
with the squared loss, let
f^{(t)}_{\textnormal{lin}}(x)=f^{(0)}(x)+{\left\langle\theta^{(t)}-\theta^{(0)},\nabla_{\theta}f^{(0)}(x)\right\rangle}
be its linearized neural network trained by the same GRW (i.e.
q_{i}^{(t)}
are the same for both networks for any
i
and
t
).
Under the conditions of
Theorem

6.6
, with a sufficiently small learning rate, for any
\delta>0
, there exist constants
\tilde{D}>0
and
C>0
such that as long as
\tilde{d}\geq\tilde{D}
, with probability at least
(1-\delta)
over random initialization we have: for any test point
x\in\mathbb{R}^{d}
such that
{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}\leq 1
,

\sup_{t\geq 0}{\left|f^{(t)}_{\textnormal{lin}}(x)-f^{(t)}(x)\right|}\leq C\tilde{d}^{-1/4}

Proof

We will use the following short-hand in the proof:

\left\{\begin{aligned} g(\theta^{(t)})&=f^{(t)}({\bm{X}})-{\bm{Y}}\\
J(\theta^{(t)})&=\nabla_{\theta}f({\bm{X}};\theta^{(t)})\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times n}\\
\Theta^{(t)}&=J(\theta^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\end{aligned}\right.

For any
\epsilon>0
, there exists
t_{\epsilon}
such that for all
t\geq t_{\epsilon}
and all
i
,
q_{i}^{(t)}\in(q_{i}-\epsilon,q_{i}+\epsilon)
.
Let
{\bm{Q}}={\textnormal{diag}}(q_{1},\cdots,q_{n})
.
Similar to
Appendix

12.A
, we can rewrite
{\bm{Q}}^{(t)}={\bm{Q}}^{(t)}_{\epsilon}=\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}
.
The update rule of GRW of wide NN is

\theta^{(t+1)}=\theta^{(t)}-\eta J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)}),

and when
t\geq t_{\epsilon}
, this can be rewritten as

\theta^{(t+1)}=\theta^{(t)}-\eta J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\left[\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right].

(44)

Next, we need three lemmas.

Lemma 12.3

(
[
147
]
, Corollary 5.35)
.

If
{\bm{A}}\in\mathbb{R}^{p\times q}
is a random matrix whose entries are independent standard normal random variables, then for every
t\geq 0
, with probability at least
1-2\exp(-t^{2}/2)
,

\sqrt{p}-\sqrt{q}-t\leq\lambda_{\min}({\bm{A}})\leq\lambda_{\max}({\bm{A}})\leq\sqrt{p}+\sqrt{q}+t.

Lemma 12.4

(Local Lipschitzness of the Jacobian)
.

There exists a constant
M>0
such that for any
C_{0}>0
and any
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists a
\tilde{D}
such that: If
\tilde{d}\geq\tilde{D}
, then with probability at least
(1-\delta)
over random initialization, for any
x
such that
{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}\leq 1
, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} \left\|\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta)-\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\tilde{\theta})\right\|_{2}&\leq\frac{M}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}\\
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta)\right\|_{2}&\leq M\\
\left\|J(\theta)-J(\tilde{\theta})\right\|_{F}&\leq\frac{M}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}\\
\left\|J(\theta)\right\|_{F}&\leq M\end{aligned}\right.,\qquad\forall\theta,\tilde{\theta}\in B(\theta^{(0)},C_{0}),

(45)

where
B(\theta^{(0)},R)={\left\{\theta:{\left\|\theta-\theta^{(0)}\right\|}_{2}<R\right\}}
.

Proof

By
Lemma

12.3
, for any
\delta\in(0,1)
, there exists
\tilde{D}>0
and
M_{1}>0
such that if
\tilde{d}\geq\tilde{D}
, then with probability at least
1-\delta
, the following holds for all
\theta
such that
{\left\|\theta-\theta^{(0)}\right\|}_{2}<C_{0}
:

\forall 0\leq l\leq L-1:{\left\|{\bm{W}}^{l}\right\|}_{2}\leq 3\sqrt{\tilde{d}};\quad{\left\|{\bm{W}}^{L}\right\|}_{2}\leq C_{0}\leq 3\tilde{d}^{1/4};\quad\forall 0\leq l\leq L:{\left\|\beta{\bm{b}}^{l}\right\|}\leq M_{1}\sqrt{\tilde{d}}.

With these inequalities, for any
x
such that
{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{h}}^{1}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}{\bm{W}}^{0}x+\beta{\bm{b}}^{0}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}\left\|{\bm{W}}^{0}\right\|_{2}\left\|x\right\|_{2}+\left\|\beta{\bm{b}}^{0}\right\|_{2}\leq(\frac{3}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}+M_{1})\sqrt{\tilde{d}};

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{h}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}{\bm{W}}^{l}{\bm{x}}^{l}+\beta{\bm{b}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\left\|{\bm{W}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\beta{\bm{b}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\qquad(\forall l\geq 1);

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\sigma({\bm{h}}^{l})-\sigma({\bm{0}}^{l})+\sigma({\bm{0}}^{l})\right\|_{2}\leq L_{0}\left\|{\bm{h}}^{l}\right\|_{2}+\sigma(0)\sqrt{\tilde{d}}\qquad(\forall l\geq 1).

Here,
L_{0}
is the Lipschitz constant of
\sigma
and
\sigma({\bm{0}}^{l})=(\sigma(0),\cdots,\sigma(0))\in\mathbb{R}^{d_{l}}
.
Thus, we can prove by induction that there exists an
M_{2}>0
such that
\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\leq M_{2}\sqrt{\tilde{d}}
and
\left\|{\bm{h}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\leq M_{2}\sqrt{\tilde{d}}
for all
l=1,\cdots,L
.

Denote
{\bm{\alpha}}^{l}=\nabla_{{\bm{h}}^{l}}f(x)=\nabla_{{\bm{h}}^{l}}{\bm{h}}^{L+1}
. For all
1\leq l\leq L
, we have
{\bm{\alpha}}^{l}={\textnormal{diag}}(\dot{\sigma}({\bm{h}}^{l}))\frac{{\bm{W}}^{l\top}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}
,
{\bm{\alpha}}^{L+1}=1
and
\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{L}\right\|_{2}=\left\|{\textnormal{diag}}(\dot{\sigma}({\bm{h}}^{L}))\frac{{\bm{W}}^{L\top}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{3}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}L_{0}
.
Since
\sigma
is
L_{0}
-Lipschitz, we have
\dot{\sigma}(x)\leq L_{0}
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
.
Thus, we can prove by induction that there exists an
M_{3}>1
such that
\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\leq M_{3}/\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}
for all
l=1,\cdots,L
(note that this is not true for
L+1
because
{\bm{\alpha}}^{L+1}=1
).

For
l=0
,
\nabla_{{\bm{W}}^{0}}f(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}{\bm{x}}^{0}{\bm{\alpha}}^{1\top}
, so
\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{W}}^{l}}f(x)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}\left\|{\bm{x}}^{0}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{1}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}M_{3}/\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}
.
And for any
l=1,\cdots,L
,
\nabla_{{\bm{W}}^{l}}f(x)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}{\bm{x}}^{l}{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}
, so
\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{W}}^{l}}f(x)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}\leq M_{2}M_{3}
. (Note that if
M_{3}>1
, then
\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{L+1}\right\|_{2}\leq M_{3}
; and since
\tilde{d}\geq 1
, there is
\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\leq M_{3}
for
l\leq L
.) Moreover, for
l=0,\cdots,L
,
\nabla_{{\bm{b}}^{l}}f({\bm{x}})=\beta{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}
, so
\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{b}}^{l}}f(x)\right\|_{2}\leq\beta M_{3}
. Thus, there exists an
M_{4}>0
, such that
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}f(x)\right\|_{2}\leq M_{4}/\sqrt{n}
. And since
\left\|{\bm{x}}_{i}\right\|_{2}\leq 1
for all
i
, so
\left\|J(\theta)\right\|_{F}\leq M_{4}
.

Next, we consider the difference in
\nabla_{\theta}f(x)
between
\theta
and
\tilde{\theta}
. Let
\tilde{f},\tilde{{\bm{W}}},\tilde{{\bm{b}}},\tilde{{\bm{x}}},\tilde{{\bm{h}}},\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}
be the function and the values corresponding to
\tilde{\theta}
. We have

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|{\bm{h}}^{1}-\tilde{{\bm{h}}}^{1}\right\|_{2}&=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}({\bm{W}}^{0}-\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{0})x+\beta({\bm{b}}^{0}-\tilde{{\bm{b}}}^{0})\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}\left\|{\bm{W}}^{0}-\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{0}\right\|_{2}\left\|x\right\|_{2}+\beta\left\|{\bm{b}}^{0}-\tilde{{\bm{b}}}^{0}\right\|_{2}\leq\left(\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}+\beta\right)\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \left\|{\bm{h}}^{l+1}-\tilde{{\bm{h}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}&=\left\|\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}{\bm{W}}^{l}({\bm{x}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l})+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}({\bm{W}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{l})\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l}+\beta({\bm{b}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{b}}}^{l})\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\left\|{\bm{W}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}+\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\left\|{\bm{W}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}+\beta\left\|{\bm{b}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{b}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\\
&\leq 3\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}+(M_{2}+\beta)\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}\qquad(\forall l\geq 1)\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}=\left\|\sigma({\bm{h}}^{l})-\sigma(\tilde{{\bm{h}}}^{l})\right\|_{2}\leq L_{0}\left\|{\bm{h}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{h}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\qquad(\forall l\geq 1).

Thus, we can prove by induction that there exists an
M_{5}>0
such that
\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\leq M_{5}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}
for all
l
.

For
{\bm{\alpha}}^{l}
, we have
{\bm{\alpha}}^{L+1}=\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{L+1}=1
, and for all
l\geq 1
,

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|{\textnormal{diag}}(\dot{\sigma}({\bm{h}}^{l}))\frac{{\bm{W}}^{l\top}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}-{\textnormal{diag}}(\dot{\sigma}(\tilde{{\bm{h}}}^{l}))\frac{\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{l\top}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}

(46)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \leq\left\|{\textnormal{diag}}(\dot{\sigma}({\bm{h}}^{l}))\frac{{\bm{W}}^{l\top}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}({\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1})\right\|_{2}&+\left\|{\textnormal{diag}}(\dot{\sigma}({\bm{h}}^{l}))\frac{({\bm{W}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{l})^{\top}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}\\
&+\left\|{\textnormal{diag}}((\dot{\sigma}({\bm{h}}^{l})-\dot{\sigma}(\tilde{{\bm{h}}}^{l})))\frac{\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{l\top}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}\end{aligned}

\displaystyle\leq 3L_{0}\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}+\left(M_{3}L_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/2}+3M_{3}M_{5}L_{1}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}\right)\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2},

where
L_{1}
is the Lipschitz constant of
\dot{\sigma}
. In particular, for
l=L
, though
\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{L+1}=1
, since
\left\|\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{L}\right\|_{2}\leq 3\tilde{d}^{1/4}
,
Equation

46
is still true. Thus, we can prove by induction that there exists an
M_{6}>0
such that
\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{M_{6}}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}
for all
l\geq 1
(note that this is also true for
l=L+1
).

Thus, for all
\theta,\tilde{\theta}\in B(\theta^{(0)},C_{0})
, and any
x
such that
{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}\leq 1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{W}}^{0}}f(x)-\nabla_{\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{0}}\tilde{f}(x)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}\left\|x{\bm{\alpha}}^{1\top}-x\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{1\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{1}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{d_{0}}}\frac{M_{6}}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2};

and for
l=1,\cdots,L
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\nabla_{{\bm{W}}^{l}}f(x)-\nabla_{\tilde{{\bm{W}}}^{l}}\tilde{f}(x)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\left\|x^{l}{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1\top}-\tilde{x}^{l}\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1\top}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{1}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\left(\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}+\left\|{\bm{x}}^{l}-\tilde{{\bm{x}}}^{l}\right\|_{2}\left\|\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}\right)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\frac{M_{2}M_{6}}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}+\frac{M_{5}M_{3}}{\sqrt{\tilde{d}}}\right)\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}.

Moreover, for any
l=0,\cdots,L
, we have

\left\|\nabla_{b^{l}}f(x)-\nabla_{\tilde{b}^{l}}\tilde{f}(x)\right\|_{2}=\beta\left\|{\bm{\alpha}}^{l+1}-\tilde{{\bm{\alpha}}}^{l+1}\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{\beta M_{6}}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}.

Combining all the above, we can see that there exists a constant
M_{7}>0
such that
\left\|\nabla_{\theta}f(x)-\nabla_{\tilde{\theta}}\tilde{f}(x)\right\|_{2}\leq\frac{M_{7}}{\sqrt{n}\cdot\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}
, so that
\left\|J(\theta)-J(\tilde{\theta})\right\|_{F}\leq\frac{M_{7}}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left\|\theta-\tilde{\theta}\right\|_{2}
.

Lemma 12.5
.

There exist constants
M>0
and
\epsilon_{0}>0
such that for all
\epsilon\in(0,\epsilon_{0}]
,
\eta\leq\eta^{*}
and any
\delta>0
, there exist
R_{0}>0
,
\tilde{D}>0
and
B>1
such that for any
\tilde{d}\geq\tilde{D}
, the following (i) and (ii) hold with probability at least
(1-\delta)
over random initialization when applying gradient descent with learning rate
\eta
:

(i)

For all
t\leq t_{\epsilon}
, there is

\displaystyle\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}\leq B^{t}R_{0}

(47)

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{t}\left\|\theta^{(j)}-\theta^{(j-1)}\right\|_{2}\leq\eta MR_{0}\sum_{j=1}^{t}B^{j-1}<\frac{MB^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}}{B-1}

(48)

(ii)

For all
t\geq t_{\epsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}\leq\left(1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}\right)^{t-t_{\epsilon}}B^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}

(49)

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} \sum_{j=t_{\epsilon}+1}^{t}\left\|\theta^{(j)}-\theta^{(j-1)}\right\|_{2}&\leq\eta\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}MB^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}\sum_{j=t_{\epsilon}+1}^{t}\left(1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}\right)^{j-t_{\epsilon}}\\
&<\frac{3\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}MB^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}}{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}\end{aligned}

(50)

Proof

Note that for any
x
,
f^{(0)}(x)=\beta{\bm{b}}^{L}
where
{\bm{b}}^{L}
is sampled from the standard Gaussian distribution. Thus, for any
\delta>0
, there exists a constant
R_{0}
such that with probability at least
(1-\delta/3)
over random initialization, we have
{\left\|g(\theta^{(0)})\right\|}_{2}<R_{0}
.
And by
Lemma

6.5
, there exists
D_{2}\geq 0
such that for any
\tilde{d}\geq D_{2}
, with probability at least
(1-\delta/3)
, we have
{\left\|\Theta-\Theta^{(0)}\right\|}\leq\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}
.

Let
M
be the constant in
Lemma

12.4
. Let
\epsilon_{0}=\frac{(q^{*}\lambda_{\min})^{2}}{108M^{4}}
,
B=1+\eta^{*}M^{2}
, and
C_{0}=\frac{MB^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}}{B-1}+\frac{3\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}MB^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}}{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}
.
By
Lemma

12.4
, there exists
D_{1}>0
such that with probability at least
(1-\delta/3)
, for any
\tilde{d}\geq D_{1}
,
Equation

45
is true for all
\theta,\tilde{\theta}\in B(\theta^{(0)},C_{0})
.
By union bound, with probability at least
1-\delta
, all the three above inequalities holds.

Let us prove
Equations

47
and
48
by induction. They are obviously true for
t=0
.
Suppose they are true for
t
.
Then, for
t+1
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\theta^{(t+1)}-\theta^{(t)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\|J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\right\|_{2}\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}\leq\eta\left\|J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\right\|_{F}\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\|J(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{F}\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}\leq M\eta B^{t}R_{0},

which means that
Equation

48
is also true for
t+1
. In terms of
Equation

47
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|g(\theta^{(t+1)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|g(\theta^{(t+1)})-g(\theta^{(t)})+g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}(\theta^{(t+1)}-\theta^{(t)})+g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\eta J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)})+g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\right\|_{2}\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\left\|\eta J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\right\|_{2}\right)\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left(1+\eta\left\|J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})\right\|_{F}\left\|J(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{F}\right)\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq(1+\eta^{*}M^{2})\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}\leq B^{t+1}R_{0}.

Hence,
Equations

47
and
48
are true for all
t\leq t_{\epsilon}
, which implies that
\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t_{\epsilon})})\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|g(\theta^{(t_{\epsilon})})\right\|_{2}\leq B^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}
.
Thus,
Equation

49
is true for
t=t_{\epsilon}
.
And
Equation

50
is obviously true for
t=t_{\epsilon}
.

Next, let us prove
Equations

49
and
50
by induction. Suppose they are true for
t
. By
Equation

44
, for
t+1
we have

\displaystyle\left\|\theta^{(t+1)}-\theta^{(t)}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\|J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\left\|J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{F}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\eta\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}\left\|J(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{F}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq M\eta\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}\left(1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}\right)^{t-t_{\epsilon}}B^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0},

which implies that
Equation

50
holds for
t+1
. In terms of
Equation

49
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t+1)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t+1)})-\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})+\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}(\theta^{(t+1)}-\theta^{(t)})+\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle=\left\|-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)})+\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\left(1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}\right)^{t}R_{0},

where
\tilde{\theta}^{(t)}
is some linear interpolation between
\theta^{(t)}
and
\theta^{(t+1)}
. Now we prove that

\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\leq 1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}.

(51)

For any unit vector
{\bm{v}}\in\mathbb{R}^{n}
, we have

{\bm{v}}^{\top}({\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}){\bm{v}}=1-\eta{\bm{v}}^{\top}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}{\bm{v}},

where
\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}{\bm{v}}\right\|_{2}\in[\sqrt{q^{*}},1]
, so for any
\eta\leq\eta^{*}
,
{\bm{v}}^{\top}({\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}){\bm{v}}\in[0,1-\eta\lambda_{\min}q^{*}]
, which implies that
\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}\leq 1-\eta\lambda_{\min}q^{*}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}+\eta\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}(\Theta-\Theta^{(0)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}+\eta\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}(J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})-J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}))\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1-\eta\lambda_{\min}q^{*}+\eta\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}(\Theta-\Theta^{(0)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{F}+\eta\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}(J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})-J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}))\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1-\eta\lambda_{\min}q^{*}+\eta\left\|\Theta-\Theta^{(0)}\right\|_{F}+\eta\left\|J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})-J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 1-\eta\lambda_{\min}q^{*}+\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}+\frac{\eta M^{2}}{\sqrt[4]{\tilde{d}}}\left(\left\|\theta^{(t)}-\theta^{(0)}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\tilde{\theta}^{(t)}-\theta^{(0)}\right\|_{2}\right)\leq 1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{2}

for all
\tilde{d}\geq\max\left\{D_{1},D_{2},\left(\frac{12M^{2}C_{0}}{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}\right)^{4}\right\}
. This implies that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{2}+\left\|\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\left(\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}-\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{2}+\eta M^{2}\sqrt{3\epsilon}\leq 1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}

holds for all
\epsilon\leq\epsilon_{0}
. Thus,
Equation

49
holds for
t+1
.

Now let us return to the proof of
Lemma

12.2
.
Choose and fix an
\epsilon
such that
\epsilon<\min\{\epsilon_{0},\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3\lambda_{\max}+q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}\right)^{2}\}
, where
\epsilon_{0}
is given by Theorem
12.5
.
Then,
t_{\epsilon}
is also fixed. There exists
\tilde{D}\geq 0
such that for any
\tilde{d}\geq\tilde{D}
, with probability at least
(1-\delta)
,
the inequalities in
Lemmas

12.4
and
12.5
hold, and
{\left\|\Theta-\Theta^{(0)}\right\|}_{F}\leq\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}
.
This implies that

{\left\|\Theta^{(0)}\right\|}_{2}\leq{\left\|\Theta\right\|}_{2}+{\left\|\Theta-\Theta^{(0)}\right\|}_{F}\leq\lambda_{\max}+\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}.

We still denote
B=1+\eta^{*}M^{2}
and
C_{0}=\frac{MB^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}}{B-1}+\frac{3\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}MB^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}}{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}
.

Lemma

12.5
guarantees that for all
t
, we have
\theta^{(t)}\in B(\theta^{(0)},C_{0})
.
Thus, we have

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta^{(0)}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}+\eta\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}(\Theta-\Theta^{(0)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 1-\eta\lambda_{\min}q^{*}+\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}=1-\frac{2\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}.

It follows that

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta^{(0)}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta^{(0)}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta^{(0)}\left(\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}-\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\right)\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq 1-\frac{2\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}+\eta(\lambda_{\max}+\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3})\sqrt{3\epsilon}.

Thus, for all
\epsilon<\frac{1}{3}\left(\frac{q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3\lambda_{\max}+q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}\right)^{2}
, we have

\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta^{(0)}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\leq 1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}.

(52)

The update rule of the GRW for the linearized neural network is:

\theta^{(t+1)}_{\textnormal{lin}}=\theta^{(t)}_{\textnormal{lin}}-\eta J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g_{\textnormal{lin}}(\theta^{(t)})

(53)

where we use the subscript “lin” to denote the linearized neural network, and with a slight abuse of notion denote
g_{\textnormal{lin}}(\theta^{(t)})=g(\theta^{(t)}_{\textnormal{lin}})
.

First, let us consider the training data
{\bm{X}}
. Denote
\Delta_{t}=g_{\textnormal{lin}}(\theta^{(t)})-g(\theta^{(t)})
. We have

\left\{\begin{aligned} g_{\textnormal{lin}}(\theta^{(t+1)})-g_{\textnormal{lin}}(\theta^{(t)})&=-\eta J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g_{\textnormal{lin}}(\theta^{(t)})\\
g(\theta^{(t+1)})-g(\theta^{(t)})&=-\eta J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)})\end{aligned}\right.

where
\tilde{\theta}^{(t)}
is some linear interpolation between
\theta^{(t)}
and
\theta^{(t+1)}
. Thus, we have

\displaystyle\Delta_{t+1}-\Delta_{t}=

\displaystyle\eta\left[J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right]{\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle-\eta J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\Delta_{t}.

By
Lemma

12.4
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\left\|\left(J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})\right)^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{F}+\left\|J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}\left(J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})\right)\right\|_{F}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle 2M^{2}C_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4},

which implies that for all
t<t_{\epsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\Delta_{t+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|\left[{\bm{I}}-\eta J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\right]\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+\left\|\eta\left[J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right]{\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{I}}-\eta J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\right\|_{F}\left\|\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+\eta\left\|J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right\|_{F}\left\|g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\eta M^{2})\left\|\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+2\eta M^{2}C_{0}B^{t}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle B\left\|\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+2\eta M^{2}C_{0}B^{t}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}.

This implies that

B^{-(t+1)}\left\|\Delta_{t+1}\right\|_{2}\leq B^{-t}\left\|\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+2\eta M^{2}C_{0}B^{-1}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}.

Since
\Delta_{0}=0
, it follows that for all
t\leq t_{\epsilon}
, we have

\left\|\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}\leq 2t\eta M^{2}C_{0}B^{t-1}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4},

and in particular, we have

\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t_{\epsilon}}\right\|_{2}\leq\left\|\Delta_{t_{\epsilon}}\right\|_{2}\leq 2t_{\epsilon}\eta M^{2}C_{0}B^{t_{\epsilon}-1}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}.

For
t\geq t_{\epsilon}
, by
Equation

44
, we have

\displaystyle\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t+1}-\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t}=

\displaystyle\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\left[J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right]\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\left[\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right]

\displaystyle-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\left[\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t}\right].

Let
{\bm{A}}={\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}={\bm{I}}-\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Theta^{(0)}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}
. Then, we have

\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t+1}={\bm{A}}\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t}+\eta\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\left[J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right]\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\left(\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right).

Let
\gamma=1-\frac{\eta q^{*}\lambda_{\min}}{3}<1
.
Combining the above with
Lemma

12.5
and
Equation

52
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t+1}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\left\|{\bm{A}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+\eta\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\left[J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right]\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}_{3\epsilon}^{(t)}}\right\|_{2}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}g(\theta^{(t)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\gamma\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+\eta\left\|J(\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-J(\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right\|_{F}\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}\gamma^{t-t_{\epsilon}}B^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}

\displaystyle\leq\;

\displaystyle\gamma\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+2\eta M^{2}C_{0}\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}\gamma^{t-t_{\epsilon}}B^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4},

which implies that

\gamma^{-(t+1)}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t+1}\right\|_{2}\leq\gamma^{-t}\left\|\sqrt{{\bm{Q}}}\Delta_{t}\right\|_{2}+2\eta M^{2}C_{0}\sqrt{1+3\epsilon}\gamma^{-1-t_{\epsilon}}B^{t_{\epsilon}}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}.

Next, we consider an arbitrary test point
x
such that
{\left\|x\right\|}_{2}\leq 1
.
Denote
\delta_{t}=f_{\textnormal{lin}}^{(t)}(x)-f^{(t)}(x)
.
Then, we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} f_{\textnormal{lin}}^{(t+1)}(x)-f_{\textnormal{lin}}^{(t)}(x)&=-\eta\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g_{\textnormal{lin}}(\theta^{(t)});\\
f^{(t+1)}(x)-f^{(t)}(x)&=-\eta\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)}).\end{aligned}\right.

Therefore, we have

\displaystyle\delta_{t+1}-\delta_{t}=

\displaystyle\eta\left[\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\tilde{\theta}^{(t)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(t)})-\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right]{\bm{Q}}^{(t)}g(\theta^{(t)})

\displaystyle-\eta\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(t)}\Delta_{t}.

For
t\leq t_{\epsilon}
, we have

\displaystyle\left\|\delta_{t}\right\|_{2}\leq

\displaystyle\eta\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\left\|\left[\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\tilde{\theta}^{(s)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(s)})-\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right]{\bm{Q}}^{(s)}\right\|_{2}\left\|g(\theta^{(s)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\eta\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\left\|\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)}){\bm{Q}}^{(s)}\right\|_{2}\left\|\Delta_{s}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\eta\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\left\|\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\tilde{\theta}^{(s)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(s)})-\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta^{(0)})^{\top}J(\theta^{(0)})\right\|_{F}\left\|g(\theta^{(s)})\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle+\eta\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}\left\|\nabla_{\theta}f(x;\theta^{(0)})\right\|_{2}\left\|J(\theta^{(0)})\right\|_{F}\left\|\Delta_{s}\right\|_{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle 2\eta M^{2}C_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}B^{s}R_{0}+\eta M^{2}\sum_{s=0}^{t-1}(2s\eta M^{2}C_{0}B^{s-1}R_{0}\tilde{d}^{-1/4}).

Hence, there exists a constant
C_{1}
such that
